Greytide tower reference

81 towers

Every tower in the game (81 registered, 75 a player can reach), generated from the code by Greytide.EditorTools.TowerReference.Write on 2026-10-30. Do not edit by hand: change the code (or the hand-read notes in Editor/TowerReferenceNotes.cs) and run it again. Lines marked (read from code) were written by reading the tower's own code; every other line is printed from the game's data, and the numbers in the read-from-code lines that come from a named constant are filled in from that constant when the reference is generated.

Glossary

How the engine treats every tower, and the words the tower pages use. Numbers are read from the rules when this page is generated.

How to read the tower pages #

  • Card units and hit points. A tower's card base is in card units (a Ballista hit is 12). A hit takes the card damage times the tower's DamageScale times Balance.Damage (0.36) off an enemy: that is the hp column. Card text and card stats are in card units.
  • The horde grows. From wave 3 every hit counts for less: it is divided by a health multiplier that rises in a straight line to 1 + 10 x the table's HpGrowth at wave 10. A boss carries 3 times that; a mini-boss carries none of that extra (Balance.MiniBossShare 0). Damage-over-time ticks ignore the growth (Balance.DotGrowth 0). The hp numbers on the tower pages are before growth.
  • Rate and range are the mini's. The game fires a tower's own attack every FireInterval seconds of its mini and reaches the mini's Range (the numbers its mini passes to its constructor). The card base's Fire Rate and Range only count as ratios: a card that adds 20% Range makes the mini reach 20% further. A tower page says so where the two disagree.
  • Height. Reach grows 3.5% for every cm the tower's eye is above the dude's feet and shrinks 1.5% per cm below, counted up to 9 cm. The eye is 2.6 cm above the tower's base.
  • Read from code. The What its code does part of each page was written by reading the tower's own code (its Fire, Update and hit hooks), not its comments. Where a comment, the tray blurb or the tag line promises something the code does not do, the Quirks say so.
  • Placeholders. In the read-from-code lines, a number that comes from a named constant in the tower's folder is printed from that constant, so it is right whenever this page is regenerated. Other numbers are literals in the code.

Where a tower's words and its code disagree #

Collected from the Quirks of the tower pages: the lines where a tower's tray blurb, tag line or code comments describe something its code does not do, or numbers its code does not use. Many are left over from issue 439, which turned several slows, holds and shoves into damage markers. Trust the code column; each tower links to its page.

TowerWhat the words say, and what the code does (read from code)
CannonThe blast calls Strike directly, not DamageAround, so a damage aura (Calculator, Captain Lightbox) does count on its splash, although the Defense comment says auras were taken off splash hits for four boosted Cannons (#163).
MageThe Blurb calls it a beam and its card base has the Beam delivery, but it fires a flying projectile.
Brother SuperglueThe mini's PadRadius (3) is not used by the game; the pad uses the line's radius.
Captain LightboxThe Blurb says 'Mind the angle'; nothing in his code depends on an angle.
The Basing GnomeIt lays no flock patch in the game: the slow-and-hide patch of the summary, the mini's notes and the Blurb's 'flock' is only called in the tower demo, and the mini's const PatchRadius (3.3) is unused in play.
The Basing GnomeSo it does not slow or hide anyone; it is a small mortar (the build line's issue 439 comment says so).
The Airbrush KidThe nozzle clogs: the first clog comes after 7 to 11 bursts, then every 8 to 13; a clogged burst does only 0.35 x the damage. The mini's summary calls the clog cosmetic, but the spec cuts the damage.
The Pepper MillThe summary comment says dudes stop and stumble back (Stagger, Push); the code does neither (issue 439 made the sneeze only a damage marker).
The Pepper MillThe Blurb says sneezing dudes take more from every other tower; the mill's own hits get the bonus too.
The Pepper MillThe comment on the half-angle speaks of a reach of 13 cm; its range is set by its line and mini (23 cm base).
Sir Cling FilmThe class summary and the snap log say the sheet sticks dudes, piles up the queue and flings them back; the code does none of this, it only hits them at the snap.
The Toaster TwinsThe class summary says the dude slides back down the road about eight cm. The code moves nobody back.
The Toaster TwinsThe landing is the full base hit of 36 card damage, not the 'little damage' the summary describes.
Spring SergeantThe summary says the dude comes down far back along the road and the dudes round it are shoved back and staggered; the code does neither.
Spring SergeantEven if ThrowBack were not 0, its share is stored and never used; the toss does not move the dude along its route.
The Lost SockThe summary comment says the dude wanders the wrong way and a boss is slowed; the code does neither (issue 439 made the sock only a damage marker).
The PlungerThe summary says it yanks a dude a long way back along the road; the code never moves the dude (issue 439 turned it into three tugs).
Shower HeadThe summary says Wet dudes are slowed a little and slide further when shoved; nothing in the code does either.
Shower HeadThe summary says a third more from Cold and Shock; the code gives +50%.
Tape MeasureThe class summary and the comment on AuraFor say 'the eight tiles round him'. The code reaches twelve tiles (AuraReach 2).
The Rules LawyerThe summary says it stops the dude in its tracks; nothing holds it (issue 439 made it a ruling). Argue (3) and BossArgue (0.8) are overwritten by 3.5 and 4 s and only appear in the log text.
The Vice AdmiralThe class summary says it 'clamps one enemy in place', slows bosses and holds others. The code holds and slows nobody.
The Spirit LevelThe blurb says slopes 'in sight'; the code checks only distance.
The Watering CanThe class summary says the soak is a short slow and the moss slows. The code slows nobody: the moss does tick damage instead (issue 439). MossSlow and BossMossSlow are unused.
The Sun CatcherNo beam ever does full card damage: unsplit hits do 60% and split hits 75%. The summary says light does full damage.
The Wind ChimeThe class summary says it shoves light dudes across the road and staggers them for Stun (0.8) s. The code does neither, and Stun and ShoveLane are unused.
Moth SquadronThe summary comment promises a slight slow; the code has none.
The Bare BulbIt no longer stops or staggers anyone (issue 439 comment): the summary's stare and boss stagger are not in the code, and the consts Stare (1.3) and Dazzle (4) are unused.
Dust Sheet GhostThe summary says grunts turn round and run back and bosses only flinch; the code turns nobody round. Every dude in the circle is only marked and struck (issue 439 comment), big or small.
Dust Sheet GhostThe 3 s 'brave' immunity in the summary is not in the code: a dude can be marked again by the very next boo.
Dust Sheet GhostThe DamageScale comment says the boo is 2 card points; the base card is 3.
The Practice AmpThe summary says the cone shoves everything back a step; the code shoves nobody. ShoveCm (36) and BigShoveK (0.4) are declared but never used.
The Kick DrumThe code works out a slow after landing (55% speed for 1.3 s, heavier dudes less) and stores it on each hop, but Land never applies it: the slow promised in the summary comment and the Blurb does not happen.
The Kick Drumw.Boss is true for every tier above grunt, so mini-bosses get the boss height, air time and (unused) slow values.
The Tangled CableThe summary says dudes trip and lie flat (1.6 s, big 0.8 s, boss 0.45 s); the code works the time out and never uses it, and no fall is drawn.
The Citronella CandleIt slows nobody: GruntSlow (0.62) and BigSlow (0.88) are declared, and the summary promises a slow, but there is no SpeedFactor in the code.
The Citronella CandleThe comment says 3 card damage a second; the stat block gives 6.
The Fly SwatterNothing in the game reads the flattened mark: the stagger the summary promises (and 0.5 s for a big one) does not happen. Flattening is only a mark.
The Fly SwatterThe comment on its base card says 30 card damage; the base card says 45.
The Hot Sauce BottleNothing slows: the summary's 55% on the line, 85% for big dudes and 80% in footprints are constants nothing uses. Footprints are only a picture (issue 439 changed the line to damage ticks).
The Hot Sauce BottleThe Blurb's burn is plain hits of the line's damage type; the code adds no burn status of its own.
The Garden GnomeThe summary says busy dudes stand still for 2.4 s; the code never holds or slows them. They keep walking, flinch, and take +35% more.
The Garden GnomeThe numbers in its comments are stale: busy lasts 3.5 s (comment 2.4 s), the re-taunt wait is 5 s after that (comment six seconds), the bonus is x1.35 (comments a fifth and 20%), and there is no one-taunt-a-second limit.
The Juice BoxThe comment on its base card says a puddle every 3.3 s; the mini fires every 3.6 s.
The Seat-Belt BuckleA boss is slowed as well as staggered, which the summary does not say.
The Road AtlasThe summary says a third more; the code gives +30%.
Remote ControlThe summary says the group turns round and walks the wrong way and a boss only hesitates; the code turns nobody (issue 439). TurnSpeed (-0.8) and BossSpeed (0.25) are never used.
The Popcorn MakerThe const Shove (0.7) is declared but never used; nothing it does shoves.
The Popcorn MakerThe Fire comment says nine kernels; the code throws 10.
The Loose ChangeThe summary comment says it is the only tower that pays for its own kills; the loot bonus is an ordinary stat that a card in the painters pack also gives.
Throw CushionThe class summary says it slows to a crawl and shoves back. The code does neither. SmotherSecs, SlowSecs and ShoveBack are declared but never used.
The Water BalloonThe class summary says a slip loses three centimetres. The code pushes 21 cm, through the engine's shove rules with their diminishing returns.
The Water BalloonThe summary says wet is only a mark that combos read. The Water Tower/Shower Head rule and the Watering Can's bonus read the same wet mark.
The SquirrelThe summary says a trip every 5 s or so; the code waits 2.6 s after each return plus the run, and does not use the mini's 5 s interval.
The BucketThe summary says only bosses are pushed less and never slowed; the code treats mini-bosses the same (IsBig).
The Sandcastle FlagThe summary says limp first, then a gust; the clock starts with the gust.
The Third Railunclear from code: the summary says it cannot stand on the track hexes. Nothing in its folder enforces this, so it depends on the board.
The TrebuchetIts default LaunchVelocity is not used: Fire aims every stone itself.
The TrebuchetThe Blurb says 'the biggest dude in reach'. The code takes any boss or above-grunt enemy first, then the farthest, not the largest.
Sir BrickalotThe BuildBase comment says a 144 lance is 12 hp; with DamageScale 1/12 and the game's x 0.36 it is about 4.3 hp.
Pint Glass Giant †The summary says the slam shoves dudes back and sideways and the band slows them; the code does neither. ShoveCm (5), WetSlow (0.55), ShoveCooldown (2.6) and the pint-shoved mark are declared but never used.
Pint Glass Giant †The BuildBase comment says 28 card damage and force 3; the stat block is 30 damage and force 0.
The Quiz Master †The summary says the crowd stops to think and the popped dude is staggered; the code holds and staggers nobody. ThinkSecs (1.2) and Stagger (1.2) are not used for any effect.
The Crisp Packet †The summary and Blurb say small dudes stop to eat; the code never holds or slows them. A bite is only damage (issue 439 comment), and the 1.1 s eating timer only tracks who is mid-mouthful.

Targeting and firing (every tower, unless its page says otherwise) #

  • Who it picks: among the living dudes inside its reach (a sphere from its eye, stretched or shrunk by height): a dude a Patient Hunter card is hunting first, then a boss or mini-boss (not one the table drives, like the parked dude train), then the nearest. A tower that prefers far takes the hunted, then a boss, then the farthest.
  • What it can see: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of static scenery and the table's own sight blockers. A tower that lobs (and is not over walls) needs the cannon's whole arc clear (8 steps). An over walls tower needs only range.
  • Flock hides: a dude more than 5 cm away standing in flock (cover above half) is hidden from towers that hit along a line (Direct); lobbers still find it. (read from code: Defense.FindTarget) No tower lays flock in play today: the Basing Gnome's patch is laid only in the tower demo (see its page), so this rule never fires.
  • Floating dudes: attacks tagged Melee or Mine never target or hit a dude a card has lifted into the air.
  • Timing: a held target's sight is re-checked every 0.1 s and a nearer target looked for every 0.15 s. The first shot at a new target waits for 0.35 s of aim, and every reload is the interval times a random 0.95 to 1.08. (the last two read from code: Defense.Update)
  • Reload with cards and auras: the mini's interval / (aura rate x the cards' rate multiplier x the line's Fire Rate over the base's, at least 0.1).
  • Reach with cards and auras: the mini's range x aura range x (the line's Range over the base's, at least 0.3) x the run's range multiplier (1) x the cards' range multiplier.
  • Card skill lines (an extra attack a Skill card adds) fire on their own clocks at their own range (or their Radius, or half the tower's range, when a card gives none) and target by the same rules.
  • Self-driven towers skip all of this: their own Update hook decides when and what they hit.
  • Auras: at most 2 aura givers count on one tower. A tower whose own attack is tagged Splash takes half an aura's fire-rate bonus, and an aura's damage bonus never applies to splash hits made through DamageAround.

What one hit does (Defense.Strike) #

Every tower hit made through Strike (directly, or through DamageAround, DamageCone, Chain, a projectile's default hit and so on) goes through these steps in order. A hit that bypasses Strike (a tower that kills or removes a dude outright, or deals hit points itself) skips them; its page says so.

  1. Floating: a Melee or Mine hit on a dude a card lifted into the air does nothing.
  2. Cards first: the card engine's pre-strike hooks may change the damage, the type and the shares.
  3. Damage: card damage, plus any of the line's conditional card bonuses that hold (target frozen, shocked, ignited, armoured, shielded, stuck, near a ledge, above or below half health), x DamageScale x Balance.Damage (0.36) x the aura's damage bonus (not on splash) x every DamageTaken hook of the towers in play (Lady Wash's coat, the Pepper Mill's sneeze, Night Light...) x the combos' multiplier x the run's Fire or Cold damage knob.
  4. Damage over time: the line's DoT share is taken off the hit and paid over 3 s instead (x the run's duration knob for its class).
  5. Armour, pierce and arcane: armour is met at Balance.ArmourScale (0.36, it follows the damage dial) of its number. Armour Pierce (card units x the tower's toy scale) and the Arcane share of the hit skip that much of it; Arcane true damage skips all of it.
  6. Growth: the result is divided by the health multiplier (see How to read).
  7. Hit floor: armour and growth together never take a hit below 10% of its damage (Balance.HitFloor), so a tower that hits always hurts.
  8. Shield: a hit that travels (a bolt, a beam, a cone, a swing; not a splash, pulse or tick) coming at the front of a shield (more than about 110 degrees from the way the dude faces) is soaked by the shield until it breaks; the rest of that hit is lost, unless the tower carries through shields. The no-shield mark, or a card that goes round shields, stops the block.
  9. Body armour: the body keeps max(25% of the hit, the hit - its armour). (read from code: Enemy.Hurt)
  10. Resistance: an enemy that resists the hit's damage class takes its ResistFactor of it.
  11. XP: the tower earns the hit points dealt, over its toy scale, as XP.
  12. If it lives: the DoT share goes into its damage-over-time pool, the statuses of the hit's type and tags land (see Statuses), and a hit that travels shoves it by the line's Force (Pull tags pull, 3x). A shove of at least 0.75 x an enemy's ragdoll impulse topples it out of the game.
  13. If it dies: the line's loot bonus and the tower's kill hooks run; the kill credit goes to the tower that fired (or the one a copied shot credits).

Delivery tags #

A tower's delivery tags are what the card filters see (a Splash support card is offered only to a tower with a Splash line). The engine itself reads only a few of them; the shape a tower really fires is its own code's (a tower tagged Splash may fire a cone). What the engine does with each tag:

TagKindWhat the engine does with itTowers tagged
Meleeshapenever targets or hits a floating dude12: Lord Gloss Coat, Spring Sergeant, The Rubber Duck, Soldering Iron Sam, The Vice Admiral, The Venus Flytrap, Moth Squadron, The Compass, The Seat-Belt Buckle, The Conker, The Squirrel, Sir Brickalot
Projectileshapenothing special (the default Fire launches a bolt)21: Ballista, Cannon, Brother Superglue, Lady Wash, The Basing Gnome, The Airbrush Kid, The Lost Sock, The Plunger, Dice Tower, The Meta Chaser, The Rotary Tool, The Watering Can, The Hot Sauce Bottle, The Juice Box, The Air Freshener, The Popcorn Maker, The Loose Change, Throw Cushion, The Water Balloon, The Trebuchet, Lego-las
Beamshapenothing special7: Mage, Old Varnish, Sister Contrast, Kettle Knight, The Sun Catcher, The Christmas Box, The Torch
Splashshapean own-attack Splash halves the aura's rate bonus for the tower32: Cannon, Brother Superglue, Lady Wash, The Basing Gnome, Sister Contrast, The Pepper Mill, Sir Cling Film, The Toaster Twins, Spring Sergeant, Captain Loofah, Shower Head, Dice Tower, The Watering Can, The Practice Amp, The Kick Drum, The Tangled Cable, The Citronella Candle, The Fly Swatter, The Hot Sauce Bottle, The Glue Stick, The Juice Box, The Air Freshener, Remote Control, The Popcorn Maker, Throw Cushion, The Water Balloon, The Bucket, The Spade, The Third Rail, The Water Tower, The Trebuchet, The Oil Pot

Statuses #

What a status does to a dude, in numbers (HitRules, Defense.SpeedFactorAt, Walker). An enemy that is immune (patience shrugging it off, or stalled) takes no slow, hold, stagger, freeze or shove at all.

StatusWhat it doesHow longStacks
Chillwalks at 55% speed; at 5 stacks it becomes Frozen (the Chill is used up)2.5 s, reset by every Chill1 a hit
Frozenstopped dead (speed 0, below every floor)1.6 s, cut by recent freezes: x (1 - seconds frozen lately / 3), the memory fading over 8 s; no refreeze for 4 s after a thawno
Shocknothing by itself: cards hit shocked dudes harder or set things off on them3 s, reset by every Shock hitno
Igniteon fire: nothing by itself; the damage is a Fire DoT share'sat least 2 s a Fire hitup to 10
Blighteach stack past the first adds 25% to the dude's damage-over-time ticksas long as its DoT, 3 sup to the line's Blight Cap (10); 3 on a boss or mini-boss
Staggerwalks at 15% speed (read from code)at least 0.9 s (or the line's Stagger length)no; a new one only runs longer
Glued (Stuck)walks at 8% speed for a grunt; bosses and mini-bosses shrug off 85% of the grip, big dudes (6 hp or more, or 1.3x size) 60% (read from code)the line's Glue seconds; after 1.8 s held in all (less by its resistance) it goes slippery and no glue holds it for 4 sno
Slow (the Slow stat)walks at (1 - Slow) of its speed; the strongest slow on it counts2 sno
Marked (cards)every tower but the one that marked it does 25% more to it6 sno
Tower marksnamed marks towers put on dudes (sneeze, coated, wet...): each tower decides what its own mark does; see Tower marks belowthe tower'sno
  • Damage over time is one pool per dude. Each hit with a DoT share adds its hit points a second to the pool and starts its timer over at that hit's length; the pool takes the newest hit's class. It ticks every 0.5 s through the body (armour cuts each tick; no shield blocks it) and is not a strike: card hit hooks never see a tick. (read from code: Walker.Burn, Defense.UpdateStatuses)
  • Bosses and mini-bosses carry less of it (#236): the pool never holds more than 1 hp a second, Blight never more than 3 stacks, and every DoT on them lasts 60% of its time.
  • A card's Blight on a dude with nothing ticking starts a tick of 0.08 hp a second of its own.

Limits on crowd control (#436, #261, #344, #548) #

  • Slows multiply to a floor. Glue, wash, flock, Chill, Slow, Stagger, Glued, combo clouds and the slowest SpeedFactor of the towers in play multiply together, but never take a grunt below 45% of its speed or a mini-boss or boss below 70%. Only Frozen (and being lifted) goes below. Two towers' SpeedFactor below 1 do not stack: the smallest counts.
  • Walking backwards: a negative SpeedFactor turns a dude round. It is outside the floor and is never sped up.
  • Speed-ups: a SpeedFactor above 1 multiplies what the slows and the floor leave; the largest counts, at most 1.5x.
  • Shoves fade: every shove (Push, Teleport back, card knockback, a combo) counts in a memory that fades over 4 s; a shove moves the dude max(0, 1 - shoves remembered / 2.6) of its distance: 1, then about 0.62, then 0.23, then nothing. A dude never loses more than 60% (a grunt) or 25% (a mini-boss or boss) of the road it walked in the last ten seconds.
  • How far a push goes: TowerApi.Push(w, n) adds a slide of n that dies away (e^-7t), so the dude moves about n / 7 cm (times the fade above). Card knockback (Force) moves the full Force in cm: scaled by the dude's weight (1 / (1 + 0.5 x (weight - 1)), at least 0.1; weight grows 6% a wave) and capped at 5 cm for a mini-boss or boss and 14 cm for anyone. (read from code: Walker.Shove, CardHost.Pushed)
  • Patience: a dude that nets less than 60% of its free walk for 12 s shrugs off knockback, holds and slows for 5 s. After 150 s alive on a table it ignores them for good.
  • Glue never holds a lane: see Glued above.

Tower marks #

The named marks the towers' code puts on dudes (api.Mark) and reads (api.Marked), found by reading the source. no-armour and no-shield are read by the engine itself (armour counts for nothing; no shield blocks). A distraction mark earns the tower that laid it decoy XP while it lasts; any other mark still makes the tower an assist when someone else kills the dude. Read by lists the code that tests the mark by a literal or constant key; a mark nobody reads does nothing but that. Towers of a retired table or dev-only towers are marked with a dagger (†).

Combos #

  • Two towers on tiles side by side (two tiles apart with a Long Rod card) whose affinities pair up make a combo by themselves. A tower is in at most 2 (more with Third Rod), nearest first. A YOLO! tower makes none.
  • A combo's strength k is 1 + the combo cards (Rod Polish, Shared Brush) of both towers, at most 2. Its cloud sits on the road tile nearest the pair, radius 7 x sqrt(k) cm; its hit effects reach 28 cm from the midpoint of the pair. (read from code)
  • A combo's extra hits go through Strike as a card's direct hit of the named tower (XP, armour and shields apply), never set off another combo, and at most 8 happen in a frame.
  • Wet towers soak: while any combo is in force anywhere on the table, every hit by a Wet-affinity tower that does not kill marks the dude wet for 4 s (Rainbow reads it). (read from code: ComboEngine.AfterStrike)
ComboPairWhat the game saysWhat the code does, at k = 1 (read from code)
SteamWet + HotA cloud over the road that slows the horde and stops its shields working.dudes in the cloud walk at 68% (at least 40% at higher k) and are marked steam and no-shield for 0.5 s, renewed every 0.2 s
CaramelHot + StickyEnemies stuck or slowed by glue burn for as long as they stay that way.every 0.5 s each held dude in reach (glued, frozen, in a glue pad or a horde patch) takes a Fire hit of 0.4 x the Hot tower's own card damage
RainbowWet + BrightA hit on a wet enemy splits in a prism and hits a second one.a hit by either tower on a wet dude that does not kill also hits the nearest other dude within 12 cm for 60% of its card damage, at most every 0.25 s
Sellotape and ScissorsSticky + SharpEnemies stuck or slowed by glue take critical hits.the pair's hits on a held dude do 2x
Pillow FightLoud + SoftEvery shove in reach also stuns.any shove of a dude in reach also staggers it for 1.3 s (once per 1.4 s per dude, mark pillow)
MotesDusty + BrightEnemies in the dust are marked and take more damage from range.dudes in the cloud are marked motes (0.7 s, renewed every 0.3 s); any tower's hit on them from more than 12 cm away does 30% more
Jack-in-the-BoxSpringy + SharpAn enemy thrown into the air is hit again as it lands.a dude in reach landing from the air takes a Physical hit of 1.3 x the Sharp tower's card damage
FlukeLucky + anythingA small chance of doubling the partner's hit, with a coin flip.15% of the pair's hits do 2x
Flash FireHot + DustyA cloud of dust rises over the road and ignites, once.when a dude comes near, dust rises; 1.5 s later everyone within 1.2 x the cloud radius takes a Fire hit of 1.1 x the Hot tower's card damage; then 7 s until the next
Belly FlopWet + SpringyA bounced enemy lands with a splash that soaks its neighbours.a dude in reach landing from the air splashes: everyone within 5.5 cm is marked wet for 5 s, slowed to 70% for 2.5 s and (but the lander) hit for 0.3 x the Wet tower's card damage

  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Projectile (default Fire): a bolt at 170 cm/s, hit radius 1.35 cm round the chest, pierces with the Pierce stat (-15% a pass).
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 170 cm/s.
  • Bot placement: reach 22 cm.
  • Rate mismatch: the card base says 1 shots/s but the mini fires every 0.9 s (1.11/s). The game uses the mini's interval; the card number only scales it (a card that adds 20% Fire Rate makes the mini 20% faster).
  • Bot reach: the bot places it as if it reached 22 cm; its mini reaches 30 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Each shot launches a bolt per repeat (the default projectile Fire) at bolt speed 170 cm/s, aimed ahead of the target.
  • The aim is scattered: up to 1.5 cm sideways each way plus up to 0.7 x the repeat number more, and -0.7 to 0.9 cm up and down, all divided by Balance.Accuracy (1 by default, -accuracy).
  • A bolt hits the first dude it meets (default OnProjectileHit) through Strike with power 1.3.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Plain default tower: f.CardDamage via Strike, so Damage and every on-hit card apply; Repeats add bolts (each with more scatter).
  • Pierces is true, so a Pierce stat lets the bolt go on through dudes (15% less damage per pass).

Quirks

  • The scatter means a share of bolts miss and sometimes hit the dude behind.
  • It is always in the tray (DraftWall.IsAlways) and is one of the three starting towers the Defense can place at the start.
  • Its kills die pinned (DeathKind.Pin).

Home table

  • Attic table: the mouse event pushes a free Ballista mini out of a hole to the table's exit, where it stands guard as a tower (costs nothing).

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: LaunchVelocity, CreateProjectile
  • Engine verbs its code calls: none
  • Shared rules it reads: Balance.Accuracy
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Cards/Packs/ConversionFamilyCards.cs, Cards/Packs/CounterFamilyCards.cs, Cards/Packs/TriggerFamilyCards.cs, Cards/Tower.cs, Game/CcWall.cs, Game/Combos/ComboCommands.cs, Game/CoverageReport.cs, Game/Defense.cs, Game/DiveCheck.cs, Game/DraftWall.cs, Game/GameLoop.cs, Game/Sight/SightCommands.cs, Game/TowerCase.cs, Game/ToyRules.cs, Minis/Tower/PaintedTower.cs, Minis/Tower/TowerFactory.cs, Tables/Attic/AtticMouse.cs, Tables/TableCheck.cs

  • Sight: lobbed: needs the cannon's arc clear from its eye to the dude's chest
  • Lobs: an arc under gravity.
  • Projectile (default Fire): lobbed shell, hit radius 1.1 cm round the chest.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 60 cm/s.
  • A hit ends the projectile whatever its Pierce.
  • Bot placement: reach 26 cm.
  • Rate mismatch: the card base says 0.5 shots/s but the mini fires every 2.2 s (0.455/s). The game uses the mini's interval; the card number only scales it (a card that adds 20% Fire Rate makes the mini 20% faster).

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Each shot lobs a ball per repeat on the cannon arc (default lob Fire), with the default random spread on repeats.
  • The ball bursts when it touches a dude in flight (HitRadius 1.1) or when it lands; a burst always ends the ball.
  • The burst radius is 6 cm times the line's Radius over the base Radius (never below 0.4 of that).
  • Every dude whose chest is within the radius plus 0.6 x its size is struck with the card damage, falling to about 66% at the edge.
  • The blast shoves with a power of 105 x (0.5 + 0.5 x Force over base Force, at least 0.5); a boss takes 0.55 of the power.

Effects

  • A survivor is flinched and slid back 14 cm x nearness (0.25 at the edge, 1 at the centre).
  • A survivor hit hard enough for its kind is toppled into a ragdoll.
  • Loose ragdoll parts and props near the blast are thrown.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Damage via card damage and Strike for each dude, so on-hit effects apply to all of them.
  • Radius cards scale the burst in proportion; Force cards raise the blast power; Repeats add balls.
  • A card rule that ignites terrain only adds an orange puff in its own code.

Quirks

  • The blast calls Strike directly, not DamageAround, so a damage aura (Calculator, Captain Lightbox) does count on its splash, although the Defense comment says auras were taken off splash hits for four boosted Cannons (#163).
  • It is always in the tray (DraftWall.IsAlways) and is one of the three starting towers the Defense can place at the start.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnProjectileHit, OnProjectileLand, CreateProjectile
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Horde.BlastWith, Strike
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Cards/Packs/ConstraintCostCards.cs, Cards/Packs/ConversionFamilyCards.cs, Cards/Packs/CounterFamilyCards.cs, Cards/Packs/EconomyRiskCards.cs, Cards/Packs/TriggerFamilyCards.cs, Cards/Tower.cs, Game/CcWall.cs, Game/Combos/ComboCommands.cs, Game/CoverageReport.cs, Game/Defense.cs, Game/DraftWall.cs, Game/GameLoop.cs, Game/Horde.cs, Game/Sight/SightCommands.cs, Game/Sight/SightRules.cs, Game/ToyRules.cs, Minis/Tower/TowerFactory.cs, Tables/TableCheck.cs

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Fires a slightly homing bolt (the default projectile Fire, one per repeat).
  • On a hit it does one Strike of the bolt's card damage.
  • If its line has Bounces, the zap then jumps to the nearest living dude not yet hit within 9 cm of the last one, each jump x0.8 the one before, one Strike per jump.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Damage is the bolt's card damage, through Strike, so on-hit cards apply to the bolt and to every chain jump.
  • Bounces (Live Wire, Conductive Paste) sets the number of chain jumps; it has none of its own.
  • Pierce lets the bolt go on through, 15% weaker each pass; a dude already hit by the chain is not hit again by the same bolt.
  • Repeats give extra bolts.

Quirks

  • Chain jumps ignore walls and sight.
  • The Blurb calls it a beam and its card base has the Beam delivery, but it fires a flying projectile.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnProjectileHit, CreateProjectile
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Strike

Brother Superglue #

T-004 · superglue · base pool · 90 points

Home
Base pool (every table) (in every table's tray)

T-004, TowerKind.Superglue

(its card pool)
Also called
Glue
Tray blurb
Daubs the road with glue. Whatever walks through it slows to a crawl. Fingers are part of the model now.
Tray tag line
STICKY PATCH SLOWS

Base attack (from the card base and the mini)

Card unitsIn play
Damage typePhysicalno status of its own
Delivery tagsProjectile, Splashwhat card filters see; the tower's code decides the real shape
Damage per hit20.36 hp (x DamageScale 1/2 x Balance.Damage 0.36)
Fire rate0.5 /severy 2.6 s (0.385 /s), the mini's own interval
Range20 cm20 cm, the mini's own range
Radius3.2 cmsplash or pulse radius, if its code reads it
  • Sight: lobbed: needs the cannon's arc clear from its eye to the dude's chest
  • Lobs: an arc under gravity.
  • Projectile (default Fire): lobbed shell, hit radius 0 cm round the chest.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 60 cm/s.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Bot placement: reach 11 cm.
  • Rate mismatch: the card base says 0.5 shots/s but the mini fires every 2.6 s (0.385/s). The game uses the mini's interval; the card number only scales it (a card that adds 20% Fire Rate makes the mini 20% faster).
  • Bot reach: the bot places it as if it reached 11 cm; its mini reaches 20 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Lobs a glob (the default projectile fire, 40 cm/s aim) that lands on the terrain.
  • Where it lands it lays a glue pad of the line's radius (at least 1.5 cm; base 3.2) and splashes DamageAround of the projectile's card damage (base 2) over the same radius.

Targeting

  • Skips a dude already standing well inside a fresh pad (pad factor below 0.75 at its feet).

Effects

  • The pad slows whoever stands within 0.85 x its radius to 52% speed, blended to nothing over the outer fifth of that.
  • The pad starts working 0.15 s after it lands, holds full grip 2.6 s, then lets go over 2.2 s; it slows nothing after 4.8 s.
  • Bosses and mini-bosses shrug off 85% of the pad's slow, big grunts (6 hp or more, or scale 1.3 or more) 60%.
  • A dude the glue grip has made slippery (after 1.8 s of holds from any tower) ignores pads for 4 s.
  • All slows together never take a grunt below 45% speed, or a boss or mini-boss below 70% (engine floor).
  • Lays a zone for its control time (NoteZone) of the pad's radius for 4.8 s.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Uses the default Fire, so Repeats throw extra globs, each laying its own pad.
  • Reads the line's Radius (line.Stats[Stat.Radius]) for both the pad and the splash, and the projectile's card damage for the splash.
  • The splash goes through Strike (via DamageAround), so card on-hit effects apply.
  • The aura damage bonus does not apply to its splash (engine rule for DamageAround).

Quirks

  • Only 10 pads exist on the table at once, shared by every Superglue; a new pad replaces the oldest.
  • Pads are the only thing that fills the glue layer (Glue.Splash); other code only reads it.
  • A pad is a drag, never a hold: at 0.52 it stays above the 0.5 test, so it never runs the glue grip clock and never gives the card Stuck status stack.
  • Card conditions on a stuck target and the combo engine count a dude as held while its pad factor is below 0.95.
  • The mini's PadRadius (3) is not used by the game; the pad uses the line's radius.
  • It is in every tray: DraftWall always offers it.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnProjectileLand, SkipTarget, CreateProjectile, PlayFireSound
  • Engine verbs its code calls: DamageAround, Glue.StickAt, NoteZone
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/CcWall.cs, Game/Combos/ComboCommands.cs, Game/CoverageReport.cs, Game/DraftWall.cs, Tables/TableCheck.cs

Stands in the road and shoves back whatever touches him. Wet look, forever.

Base attack (from the card base and the mini)

Card unitsIn play
Damage typePhysicalno status of its own
Delivery tagsMelee, Knockbackwhat card filters see; the tower's code decides the real shape
Damage per hit40.576 hp (x DamageScale 0.4 x Balance.Damage 0.36)
Fire rate0.5 /severy 2 s (0.5 /s), the mini's own interval
Range6 cm6 cm, the mini's own range
Force1212 cm shove for a grunt from a hit that travels (scaled by weight), if its code strikes with travel
Radius5.5 cmsplash or pulse radius, if its code reads it
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 60 cm/s.
  • Stands on the road as well as on build tiles.
  • No coverage goal: it stands on the road or only buffs, so the table check does not ask it to reach the road.
  • Bot placement: reach 6 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Each fire is a shield bash: a fan from 1.2 cm above his base along the flat shot direction, radius the larger of 5.5 cm and the line's Radius stat (plus 0.6 x each dude's scale), half-angle 70 degrees.
  • Each dude in the fan that has not broken through him gets the bash's shove: a slide along its road of 0.55 x the push (55 cm/s away from him, 1 to 0.5 x from near to far edge).
  • A dude whose enemy type has a ragdoll impulse is toppled by that shove instead, and is not struck.
  • Every other dude in the fan takes one Strike of the shot's card damage, directed away from him (power 0.8).

Targeting

  • He stands on the road. Dudes are hit within the fan whether they are the target or not.

Effects

  • Block: a dude within 2.2 cm plus 1.2 x its scale of him moves at 30% speed until it breaks through.
  • Break-through clock: a dude breaks through after 3 s next to him; each other dude on its route up to 12 cm behind it speeds the clock by +50%.
  • A dude that has broken through is no longer slowed or shoved by him (it is still struck); he forgets it once it is dead or more than 8 cm away.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads the shot's card damage (Damage stat) and the line's Radius stat (only to widen the fan beyond 5.5 cm). Repeats are ignored.
  • Hits go through Strike, so card on-hit effects apply and the line's Force knocks dudes away from him.

Quirks

  • The shove only reaches 5.5 cm (the bash effect's own radius), so a Radius card widens the damage but not the shove.
  • A toppled dude takes no damage and no card on-hit effects from that bash.
  • The block is a speed factor, not a hold, so the engine's glue rules do not cap it; his own clock does.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Update, Fire, SpeedFactor
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Horde.Topple, Strike
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/Combos/ComboCommands.cs, Game/CoverageReport.cs

Radius2.8 cmsplash or pulse radius, if its code reads it
  • Sight: lobbed: needs the cannon's arc clear from its eye to the dude's chest
  • Lobs: an arc under gravity.
  • Projectile (default Fire): lobbed shell, hit radius 0 cm round the chest.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 60 cm/s.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Modifies damage: its DamageTaken hook multiplies every tower's hits (see its note).
  • Bot placement: reach 20 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Lobs a blob of wash (the default projectile Fire, one lob per repeat) that only lands on terrain; it never hits a dude in the air.
  • Where it lands it lays a wash puddle of the line's radius (at least 1.5 cm) and coats every dude within that radius plus half its scale.
  • Then it deals DamageAround at the same point and radius with the shot's card damage (full at the centre, 40% at the edge).

Effects

  • Coat: the mark coated for 4 s; while it is on, every hit from every tower (her own included) does x1.25 (+25%).
  • Puddles keep coating: once a frame, every dude standing inside a live puddle (spread for 0.2 s, not yet 6 s old, inside about 90% of its radius) has its coat renewed for 4 s.
  • Puddles do not slow (the wash slow returns 1 now).
  • The game keeps at most 12 puddles at once across all Lady Washes; a new one replaces the oldest.
  • Each landing is noted as a control zone for 6 s.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Puddle and splash radius is the line's Radius stat, at least 1.5 cm.
  • Damage is the projectile's card damage, dealt by DamageAround, which calls Strike per dude, so on-hit cards apply; as a splash it gets no Captain Lightbox damage bonus.
  • Repeats give extra lobs with random spread; Range and FireRate work through the engine.
  • Her delivery has Splash, so a Captain Lightbox gives her only half the fire-rate bonus.

Quirks

  • The coat does not stack: only the first placed Lady Wash in the slot list answers for coated, so two of her still give x1.25; the mark itself can come from any of them.
  • The coat multiplies everything, including DoT ticks and her own splash.
  • Bosses and mini-bosses are coated like anyone else.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update, OnProjectileLand, DamageTaken, CreateProjectile, PlayFireSound
  • Engine verbs its code calls: DamageAround, Mark, Marked, NoteZone
  • Marks it puts on dudes: coated
  • Marks it reads: coated
  • Named outside its folder in: Game/CcWall.cs, Game/Combos/ComboCommands.cs, Game/CoverageReport.cs, Game/DraftWall.cs, Run/RunData.cs, Tables/TableCheck.cs

Tray blurb
Never shoots. Boosts the towers on the eight tiles round him. Mind the angle.
Tray tag line
BUFFS ALLIES

Base attack (from the card base and the mini)

Card unitsIn play
Damage typePhysicalno status of its own
Delivery tagsPulsewhat card filters see; the tower's code decides the real shape
Damage per hit0none
Fire rate0.28 /severy 3.6 s (0.278 /s), the mini's own interval
Range4 cm4 cm, the mini's own range
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it; its Update hook runs it.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Aura reach: 1.5 cells, centre to centre (diagonals too).
  • No coverage goal: it stands on the road or only buffs, so the table check does not ask it to reach the road.
  • Bot placement: reach 4 cm, beside other towers.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • He never attacks: every 3.6 s (the mini's own fire interval, not the card fire rate) he plays his fire pose and a light pulse runs over his tile. The pulse does nothing.

Targeting

  • Self-driven: he never aims or picks a target.

Effects

  • Aura: every placed tower within 1.5 cells centre to centre (the eight tiles round him on a square board) gets +15% damage, +20% fire rate and +10% range, all the time.
  • Two boxes on one tower add up (+30% damage, +40% rate, +20% range), but at most 2 aura givers of any kind count on one tower (CardsConfig.AuraCap); the first ones in the slot list win.
  • The damage bonus applies to Strike hits but not to splash hits dealt through DamageAround (Lady Wash, for one).
  • A tower whose first line has the Splash delivery gets only half the fire-rate bonus.
  • The range bonus multiplies the receiver's reach for its base line and its card lines.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • His attack line is not read at all: the buff numbers are fixed constants in LightboxAura, so damage, rate and range cards on him change nothing.
  • Support keystones on him do change it: Long Reach adds a second ring of tiles, Overclock limits it to edge neighbours with a bonus, the keystone's buff strength scales the numbers, and Rally Cry adds fire rate while it sounds.

Quirks

  • He buffs any placed tower in reach, including other boxes and towers that never attack, and each of those still uses one of the 2 aura slots.
  • A tower with the YOLO keystone can never be buffed.
  • He does not check whether towers may shoot (CanShoot): the pulse plays during hold-fire too.
  • The Blurb says 'Mind the angle'; nothing in his code depends on an angle.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotAdded, OnSlotRemoved, Update, AuraFor
  • Engine verbs its code calls: none
  • Named outside its folder in: Game/Combos/ComboCommands.cs, Game/CoverageReport.cs

Radius3.3 cmsplash or pulse radius, if its code reads it
  • Sight: lobbed: needs the cannon's arc clear from its eye to the dude's chest
  • Lobs: an arc under gravity.
  • Projectile (default Fire): lobbed shell, hit radius 0 cm round the chest.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 60 cm/s.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Bot placement: reach 12 cm.
  • Bot reach: the bot places it as if it reached 12 cm; its mini reaches 20 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Each shot lobs a clump of flock per repeat on the cannon arc (default lob Fire), with the default random spread on repeats.
  • Where a clump lands it splashes card damage with DamageAround over the line's Radius (at least 1.5 cm).

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Damage via the projectile's card damage and DamageAround (Strike per dude), so on-hit cards apply; Radius cards widen the splash; Repeats add clumps.
  • As a lobber its clump only lands on terrain (HitRadius 0), never on a dude in flight.

Quirks

  • It lays no flock patch in the game: the slow-and-hide patch of the summary, the mini's notes and the Blurb's 'flock' is only called in the tower demo, and the mini's const PatchRadius (3.3) is unused in play.
  • So it does not slow or hide anyone; it is a small mortar (the build line's issue 439 comment says so).

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnProjectileLand, CreateProjectile, PlayFireSound
  • Engine verbs its code calls: DamageAround

Tray blurb
A fast, short spray that shreds crowds up close. Has never cleaned the nozzle.

Base attack (from the card base and the mini)

Card unitsIn play
Damage typePhysicalno status of its own
Delivery tagsProjectilewhat card filters see; the tower's code decides the real shape
Damage per hit20.108 hp (x DamageScale 0.15 x Balance.Damage 0.36)
Fire rate7.7 /severy 0.13 s (7.69 /s), the mini's own interval
Range20 cm20 cm, the mini's own range
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Aims ahead for a shot at instant speed.
  • Bot placement: reach 8 cm.
  • Bot reach: the bot places it as if it reached 8 cm; its mini reaches 20 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Each burst sprays a cone from the nozzle: reach is 20 cm scaled by the line's Range against the base Range and by the tower's range aura (at least 1 cm).
  • The cone's half-angle is 0.42 rad (about 24 degrees, the mist look's 0.3 rad plus 0.12).
  • It hits only the nearest 3 dudes in the cone that it can see (terrain sight check per dude), nearest first (Balance.AirbrushMaxHits, -airbrushcap).
  • Each hit goes through Strike at the card damage times a fade that drops linearly to 60% at the far end of the cone (Balance.AirbrushFalloff 0.4, -airbrushfall).
  • The mini fires every 0.13 s with a reach of 20 cm.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Damage is f.CardDamage, so Damage cards apply; every hit goes through Strike, so on-hit card effects apply to each of the up to 3 dudes.
  • Range cards lengthen the cone in proportion (line Range over base Range); it does not call api.RangeOf, so unclear from code whether the run's range multiplier reaches the cone.
  • Repeats are ignored: the Fire override makes one burst per shot.
  • Radius, Force and Pierce are not read by its own code.

Quirks

  • The nozzle clogs: the first clog comes after 7 to 11 bursts, then every 8 to 13; a clogged burst does only 0.35 x the damage. The mini's summary calls the clog cosmetic, but the spec cuts the damage.
  • It swaps paint cup every 9 bursts; the colour is cosmetic only.
  • The 3-hit cap is per burst, so a crowd at the nozzle takes the same as 3 dudes.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Fire
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Blocked, Strike
  • Shared rules it reads: Balance.AirbrushFalloff, Balance.AirbrushHit, Balance.AirbrushMaxHits
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Fx/AirbrushFx.cs, Game/CoverageReport.cs, Run/RunData.cs

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • After the beam wind-up (1 s on the mini) it fires an instant beam from the muzzle along its aim, traced up to 46 cm or the first terrain in the way.
  • It hits the dudes nearest the muzzle along that line (within 1.1 x their size of it), 1 plus the line's Pierce of them.
  • Each gets one Strike with travel along the beam: the first the full card damage, each next one x0.7 the one before.
  • The beam leaves a glossy sheen where it ends; the sheen is only a look.

Targeting

  • It may hit dudes other than its target that stand nearer along the line.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Damage is f.CardDamage through Strike, so on-hit cards apply.
  • Pierce adds one more dude per point, each x0.7.
  • Repeats are ignored: one beam per shot.

Quirks

  • The beam is traced only 46 cm: with range from cards, auras or high ground its target can be further than that, and then the beam stops short.

Radius3 cmsplash or pulse radius, if its code reads it
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Beam: an instant hit once its mini is charged.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at instant speed.
  • Bot placement: reach 26 cm.
  • Bot reach: the bot places it as if it reached 26 cm; its mini reaches 38 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • A beam tower: after a 1.4 s wind-up she fires one stroke from the muzzle along the aim.
  • The stroke is a sphere cast 36 cm long that stops at the first dude, prop, ragdoll or terrain it touches; without a hit it ends 36 cm out (or where it meets terrain).
  • Where the stroke ends, every dude within the radius (flat, plus half its scale) is hit, and so is every dude within 0.7 x its size of the stroke itself.
  • Each of them takes one Strike of the card damage (base 60), with no falloff, travelling along the stroke.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads f.CardDamage and the line's Radius (line.Stats[Stat.Radius], at least 2 cm; base 3).
  • Hits go through Strike, so card on-hit effects apply.
  • Ignores Repeats: one stroke per shot.
  • Range cards lengthen her targeting reach but not the stroke, which is always 36 cm.

Quirks

  • Her reach (38 cm) is longer than the 36 cm stroke, so a target beyond 36 cm is not reached: the burst lands short of it.
  • The burst centres on the first dude the stroke touches, which need not be the target.
  • The hits travel along the stroke, so a dude facing her can block with a front shield.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Ready, Fire
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Strike, Sweep
  • Shared rules it reads: Balance.ContrastHit
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/CoverageReport.cs, Run/RunData.cs

PEPPERS A CONE

  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Aims ahead for a shot at instant speed.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Modifies damage: its DamageTaken hook multiplies every tower's hits (see its note).
  • Bot placement: reach 14 cm.
  • Bot reach: the bot places it as if it reached 14 cm; its mini reaches 23 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Grinds a cone from the muzzle along its aim: half-angle 33 degrees (66 degrees across, widened for each dude's size), as long as its range.
  • Every dude in the cone that terrain does not hide takes one Strike of the card damage with no travel direction.
  • Every survivor is marked sneeze for 3.5 s.

Effects

  • While sneeze is on, every hit from every tower, the mill's own included, does x1.25 (+25%).
  • sneeze is a decoy mark: the tower earns decoy time for it.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Cone length is api.RangeOf; the Radius stat is not read.
  • Damage is f.CardDamage through Strike, so on-hit cards apply.
  • Repeats are ignored: one cone per shot.
  • Its delivery is Splash, so a Captain Lightbox gives it half the fire-rate bonus; the damage bonus does apply.

Quirks

  • The summary comment says dudes stop and stumble back (Stagger, Push); the code does neither (issue 439 made the sneeze only a damage marker).
  • The Blurb says sneezing dudes take more from every other tower; the mill's own hits get the bonus too.
  • Each Pepper Mill on the table applies its own multiplier, so two mills make a sneezing dude take x1.56.
  • The comment on the half-angle speaks of a reach of 13 cm; its range is set by its line and mini (23 cm base).

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Fire, DamageTaken
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Blocked, Mark, Marked, RangeOf, Strike
  • Marks it puts on dudes: sneeze
  • Marks it reads: sneeze
  • Named outside its folder in: Game/CcWall.cs, Game/Combos/ComboCommands.cs

  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Beam: an instant hit once its mini is charged.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at instant speed.
  • Bot placement: reach 18 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • A beam: it charges for 1.5 s, then fires one straight jet along the shot direction, 1.15 x its range long, cut short where it meets terrain.
  • The first 1 + Pierce dudes along the jet (within 1.15 x their size of it, nearest first) each take one Strike of the shot's card damage with no travel direction (power 0.8).

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads the shot's card damage (Damage stat), Range via RangeOf (x1.15 for the jet) and the Pierce stat (dudes hit = 1 + Pierce). Repeats are ignored.
  • Hits go through Strike, so card on-hit effects, its damage type and the line's DotShare apply; with no travel direction a shield never blocks it.

Quirks

  • Dudes beyond the first 1 + Pierce on the line take nothing.
  • Nothing in the jet checks sight past the terrain sweep; any dude near the segment counts.

Home table

  • On the kitchen, when the line's damage class is Fire, a dude standing in the pan's steam (or the steam cloud) takes the steam's Fire multiplier (x1.2) on the jet.
  • When a jet hits anyone in steam, the kettle pops 'STEAM' over itself, at most every 1.5 s; the hits, steamed hits and pops are counted for the perk check.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Ready, Fire
  • Engine verbs its code calls: AlongLine, OnHomeTable, RangeOf, Strike, Sweep

  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Aims ahead for a shot at instant speed.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Bot placement: reach 13 cm.
  • Bot reach: the bot places it as if it reached 13 cm; its mini reaches 22 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • On the engine's fire, if no sheet is up, he stretches a sheet across the target's road 2.8 cm ahead of the target (at most 3 cm before the road's end).
  • If that point is more than 1.1 x his range from the tower, the sheet goes 1.2 cm ahead of the target instead.
  • The sheet snaps when it has stood 2.4 s, when a dude with the boss flag is within 1.5 cm behind to 0.9 cm past it, or when 3 or more other dudes are in that window.
  • On the snap, every non-boss dude on the same road from 9 cm behind the sheet to 0.9 cm past it takes one Strike of the line's card damage (base 9), with no direction.
  • The sheet disappears 0.4 s after the snap; only then can he fire again.

Targeting

  • Ready is false while a sheet is up, so he keeps one sheet at a time.

Effects

  • None: the sheet neither slows nor holds anybody. SpeedFactor returns 1 in every case (issue 439).

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads the line's Damage (line.Stats[Stat.Damage], taken at the snap, not f.CardDamage) and Range (api.RangeOf, only to pull the sheet in).
  • The snap hits go through Strike, so card on-hit effects apply.
  • Ignores Repeats and the line's Radius (base 5): the snap zone is always the fixed 9 cm stretch of road.

Quirks

  • The class summary and the snap log say the sheet sticks dudes, piles up the queue and flings them back; the code does none of this, it only hits them at the snap.
  • Dudes with the boss flag (bosses, and mini-bosses spawned as such) are never hit by the snap, and one walking into the sheet snaps it at once.
  • The snap hits every non-boss dude in the zone at full card damage, with no falloff and no cap on numbers.
  • Only dudes on the sheet's own route count; a dude on another road at the same spot is ignored.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update, Ready, Fire, SpeedFactor
  • Engine verbs its code calls: RangeOf, Strike
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/CcWall.cs

TOSSES THE MARK

Base attack (from the card base and the mini)

Card unitsIn play
Damage typePhysicalno status of its own
Delivery tagsSplash, Pulsewhat card filters see; the tower's code decides the real shape
Damage per hit361.08 hp (x DamageScale 1/12 x Balance.Damage 0.36)
Fire rate0.294 /severy 3.4 s (0.294 /s), the mini's own interval
Range21 cm21 cm, the mini's own range
Radius2.4 cmsplash or pulse radius, if its code reads it
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Projectile (default Fire): a bolt at 95 cm/s, hit radius 1.35 cm round the chest.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Bot placement: reach 10 cm.
  • Bot reach: the bot places it as if it reached 10 cm; its mini reaches 21 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Runs itself in Update. It never uses the engine's aim or fire, though it does not set SelfDriven.
  • It keeps a mark of radius 2.4 cm on the road and pops every 3.4 s. The mark is shown as a disc that heats up over the cycle.
  • At the pop, every dude within 2.4 cm of the mark plus half its scale, measured flat, is affected.
  • Grunts are launched: for 0.62 s the body is lifted (up to about 5 cm) and flipped. When it lands it takes the line's full card damage through Strike, as an area hit.
  • Bosses, mini-bosses and any enemy above grunt tier are not launched. They take 0.5x the line's card damage at once.
  • No pop happens until a valid mark has been found. The reload is reset to 3.4 s when the tower is placed or moves.

Targeting

  • The mark is a point on any road between 2.5 and 17 cm from the tower (flat), sampled every 0.5 cm along each route.
  • Each point is scored as crowd within 2.4 cm x 10, minus its distance from 6 cm. The best score wins.
  • The mark is re-picked every 0.4 s during the first 65% of the cycle. After that it holds still until the pop.
  • No sight check.

Effects

  • The launch is visual only: the dude keeps walking at its real position the whole time. It is not held, slowed, stopped or moved back.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads only line.Stats[Stat.Damage], at the pop for big dudes and at the landing for grunts.
  • The radius (2.4 cm), the 2.5 to 17 cm band and the cycle (3.4 s) are constants, so Range, Radius, FireRate, Repeats and range or rate auras do nothing.
  • Hits go through Strike with no direction: card on-hit effects apply, and Force gives no knockback.

Quirks

  • It does not check api.CanShoot.
  • A launched dude can still be hit by every other tower during its flight. Nothing in the code makes it untargetable.
  • The class summary says the dude slides back down the road about eight cm. The code moves nobody back.
  • The landing is the full base hit of 36 card damage, not the 'little damage' the summary describes.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Strike
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/CcWall.cs

The Hot Water Bottle #

T-016 · hot-water-bottle · bed · 100 points

Bottle, bottle, hwb
Tray blurb
Never shoots. Put it between your towers: they fire faster, and what they hit goes warm.
Tray tag line
WARMS NEIGHBOURS

Base attack (from the card base and the mini)

Card unitsIn play
Damage typeFireevery hit Ignites
Delivery tagsPulsewhat card filters see; the tower's code decides the real shape
Damage per hit0none
Fire rate0.3 /severy 3.2 s (0.313 /s), the mini's own interval
Range4 cm4 cm, the mini's own range
Radius4 cmsplash or pulse radius, if its code reads it
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it; its Update hook runs it.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Modifies damage: its DamageTaken hook multiplies every tower's hits (see its note).
  • Aura reach: 1.5 cells, centre to centre (diagonals too).
  • No coverage goal: it stands on the road or only buffs, so the table check does not ask it to reach the road.
  • Bot placement: reach 4 cm, beside other towers.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • It never fires. Every 3.2 s (the mini's interval) it plays a puff and a ring over its neighbours, for show.

Targeting

  • Self-driven: it never aims.

Effects

  • Aura: towers on the eight tiles round it fire +30% faster; another Hot Water Bottle gets nothing.
  • Warmth: every hit by a tower on those eight tiles (not another bottle) sets a burn of 0.15 hp per second for 2.5 s on the target, credited to the tower that hit.
  • The warm dude is marked warm for 2.5 s.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • It never fires its line, so cards that change its Damage, Range, Radius or FireRate do nothing; the aura and burn numbers are constants.

Quirks

  • The burn uses the walker's one damage-over-time slot: it keeps the larger rate and longer time already there, but sets the class to Fire and the owner to the hitter, so it can turn another tower's DoT into a Fire one.
  • Two bottles next to one tower do not stack the burn (the larger rate wins); the boss DoT caps still apply.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Update, AuraFor, DamageTaken
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Mark, Marked
  • Marks it puts on dudes: warm
  • Marks it reads: warm
  • Named outside its folder in: Game/CoverageReport.cs

Spring Sergeant #

T-017 · spring-sergeant · bed · 120 points

Home
The Bed (in that table's tray)

120 points

shooter (its card pool)
Also called
Sergeant, sergeant, spring
Tray blurb
Boings the nearest dude straight up. The landing hurts it and everyone round it. Put him at a bunch.
Tray tag line
BOINGS THEM UP

Base attack (from the card base and the mini)

Card unitsIn play
Damage typePhysicalno status of its own
Delivery tagsMelee, Splashwhat card filters see; the tower's code decides the real shape
Damage per hit121.08 hp (x DamageScale 1/4 x Balance.Damage 0.36)
Fire rate0.36 /severy 2.8 s (0.357 /s), the mini's own interval
Range15 cm15 cm, the mini's own range
Radius4.5 cmsplash or pulse radius, if its code reads it
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Bot placement: reach 14 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • On each shot he thumps one dude: a Strike of the card damage (base 12) travelling from him to it.
  • If it survives and was not just thrown, it gets the mark sprung for 0.85 + 4 s and is tossed: its model is lifted and spun, a picture only.
  • The toss lasts 0.85 s, or 0.7 x that for a big dude or a boss.
  • When the toss ends, the thrown dude takes a Strike of 0.8 x the card damage, and every other dude within 4.5 cm of it takes 0.64 x the card damage; neither has a direction.

Targeting

  • If the engine's target carries sprung, he takes the nearest dude without the mark that is in range of the muzzle and in sight.
  • If every dude in range is marked, he thumps the engine's target but does not toss it.

Effects

  • Mark sprung for 0.85 + 4 s: a dude is not tossed again meanwhile.
  • No throw-back, push or stagger: 0, 0 and 0 are all 0, and the code never moves the walker.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Reads f.CardDamage and Range (api.RangeOf, for picking another dude).
  • All hits go through Strike, so card on-hit effects apply.
  • Ignores Repeats and the line's Radius: the landing radius is always 4.5 cm.

Quirks

  • The summary says the dude comes down far back along the road and the dudes round it are shoved back and staggered; the code does neither.
  • Even if ThrowBack were not 0, its share is stored and never used; the toss does not move the dude along its route.
  • A tossed dude keeps walking and can be hit by other towers during the toss.
  • Heavy dudes (0.3 x height) and bosses (0.12 x height) only bob; the difference is visual.
  • The landing radius is measured in 3D from the thrown dude's position.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Update, Fire
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Blocked, Mark, Marked, RangeOf, Strike
  • Marks it puts on dudes: sprung
  • Marks it reads: sprung
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/CcWall.cs, Game/Combos/ComboCommands.cs

Tray blurb
Throws itself over the biggest dude in reach. Blindfolded, it takes far more from every other tower. Slow to reload.
Tray tag line
BLINDS THE BIG ONE

Base attack (from the card base and the mini)

Card unitsIn play
Damage typePhysicalno status of its own
Delivery tagsProjectilewhat card filters see; the tower's code decides the real shape
Damage per hit20.12 hp (x DamageScale 1/6 x Balance.Damage 0.36)
Fire rate0.16 /severy 6.2 s (0.161 /s), the mini's own interval
Range25 cm25 cm, the mini's own range
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 70 cm/s.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • A hit ends the projectile whatever its Pierce.
  • Modifies damage: its DamageTaken hook multiplies every tower's hits (see its note).
  • Bot placement: reach 22 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Picks its own target: of the dudes in range and in sight that are not marked sock-spent, the one with the most max hp (a boss or mini-boss counts a quarter); ties go to the one furthest along the road.
  • Throws one sock at 70 cm/s, aimed ahead and homing slightly; the sock ends on its first hit.
  • On a hit it does one Strike of the card damage on the picked dude (or on whoever it touched, if the picked one is gone or more than 4 cm away).
  • If the dude survives and is not sock-spent, the sock goes on: sock for 4 s on an ordinary dude, 6 s on a heavy grunt (6 hp or more, or scale 1.3 or more), 5 s on a boss or mini-boss.

Targeting

  • Ignores the engine's target unless nobody else qualifies: the biggest max hp in range and sight, bosses and mini-bosses weighted at a quarter.
  • Skips dudes marked sock-spent (wearing a sock, or shook one off less than 4 s ago); if all are, it throws at the engine's target anyway.

Effects

  • While sock is on, every hit from every tower does x1.5 to an ordinary or heavy dude and x1.25 to a boss or mini-boss.
  • sock-spent lasts the sock time plus 4 s, so the same dude cannot be socked again until then.
  • sock is a decoy mark: the tower earns decoy time for it.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Range is api.RangeOf, used for its own target pick.
  • Damage is f.CardDamage through Strike, so on-hit cards apply.
  • Repeats are ignored (one sock per shot); Pierce does nothing because a hit ends the sock.

Quirks

  • The summary comment says the dude wanders the wrong way and a boss is slowed; the code does neither (issue 439 made the sock only a damage marker).
  • Each Lost Sock on the table applies its own multiplier, so two socks make a socked grunt take x2.25; Lady Wash and Night Light guard against this, the sock does not.
  • The throw's own hit lands before the sock goes on, so it is not boosted.
  • w.Boss is true for every tier above grunt, so mini-bosses get the boss time and the smaller multiplier.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Fire, OnProjectileHit, OnProjectileLand, DamageTaken, CreateProjectile
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Blocked, Launch, Mark, Marked, RangeOf, Strike
  • Marks it puts on dudes: sock, sock-spent
  • Marks it reads: sock, sock-spent
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/CcWall.cs

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • It never attacks: every mini fire interval it plays its fire pose and a ring the size of its glow flashes. The pulse does nothing.

Targeting

  • Self-driven: it never aims.

Effects

  • Glow: every dude within 24 cm (flat distance from the light, height and walls ignored) takes x1.15 (+15%) from every hit of every tower except Night Lights, DoT ticks included.
  • Two lights over one dude do not stack: only the light with the lowest slot index counts.
  • Lit dudes get a golden halo and the mark lit for 0.3 s, renewed each frame.
  • It is an aura field for the support keystones: with Spotlight it slows dudes in the glow, with Lingering Mark the bonus stays on a dude after it leaves.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Its attack line is not read: the glow radius and bonus are constants, so damage, range and rate cards do nothing.
  • Support keystones do: Deep Mark and Wide Field scale the glow radius, and the field keystones scale the bonus.

Quirks

  • Nothing in the game reads the lit mark.
  • It stacks with Lady Wash's coat, the Lost Sock and the Pepper Mill's sneeze, which are separate multipliers.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update, DamageTaken, InField, FieldTakes
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Mark
  • Marks it puts on dudes: lit
  • Named outside its folder in: Game/CoverageReport.cs, Game/TowerFacts.cs

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Fires the default projectile (a rubber cup) at 120 cm/s; a hit ends the cup whatever its Pierce.
  • If the dude hit already carries the plunged mark, the cup slides off: a puff, no damage, nothing else.
  • Otherwise it marks the dude plunged for 12 s (not refreshed by later hits) and strikes it for the full card damage.
  • If the dude survives, three tugs follow at 0.9, 1.8 and 2.7 s, each a strike for 0.45 x the cup's card damage.

Effects

  • No movement effect: the yank distance is computed (20 cm x speed/5 clamped 0.8 to 1.8, x0.2 for a boss) and only written to the log; no Push or Pull is called.
  • The plunged mark makes the dude immune to this tower's cups (all Plungers share the key) for 12 s.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Cup damage is the projectile's card damage (f.CardDamage, x RepeatDamage for repeat cups); tugs are 0.45 x that.
  • Repeats fire extra cups through the default Fire (fanned 6 degrees per repeat); Pierce does nothing because a hit always ends the cup.
  • The first hit goes through Strike travelling along the cup's flight, so card on-hit effects and Force apply; the three tugs are Strikes with no direction (no knockback, shields never block them).

Quirks

  • The summary says it yanks a dude a long way back along the road; the code never moves the dude (issue 439 turned it into three tugs).
  • A plunged dude takes nothing at all from any Plunger for 12 s, so a repeat cup or a second Plunger on the same dude is wasted.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnProjectileHit, CreateProjectile, PlayFireSound
  • Engine verbs its code calls: After, Mark, Marked, Strike
  • Marks it puts on dudes: plunged
  • Marks it reads: plunged
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs, Game/CcWall.cs, Tables/GameStore/GameStoreBell.cs

Scrubs armour and shields off the horde, so everyone else's hits count.

STRIPS ARMOUR

Card unitsIn play

Fire rate2.4 /severy 0.42 s (2.38 /s), the mini's own interval
Range14 cm14 cm, the mini's own range
  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers; it hits along a line, so flock would hide dudes from it (no tower lays flock in play today)
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • Support: holds, slows or marks rather than kills (the tower check only asks that it damages a grunt).
  • Modifies damage: its DamageTaken hook multiplies every tower's hits (see its note).
  • Bot placement: reach 13 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Each scrub hits every dude in a flat fan in front of him: within the line's range (api.RangeOf) plus half the dude's size, and within 38 degrees of his aim either side.
  • Before each hit it strips armour (no-armour) and shield (no-shield) for 3.5 s, so his own hit already ignores both.
  • Each hit is the full card damage through Strike, pushed away from him.

Targeting

  • There is no sight check on the fan: walls and hills do not shield anyone in it.

Effects

  • A scrubbed dude is marked scrubbed for 3.5 s, and hits on it from every other tower are multiplied by 1.2 (+20%).
  • Hits from any Captain Loofah get no bonus, and several Loofahs do not stack: only the first placed one in the slot list answers.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Damage via f.CardDamage and Strike, so on-hit effects apply to every dude in the fan; Range cards lengthen the fan via api.RangeOf.
  • Repeats, Radius and Pierce are not read by its code. Its line is a Splash delivery, so it gets only half of a fire-rate aura.

Quirks

  • The fan angle (38 degrees) is fixed in code.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: Fire, DamageTaken, PlayFireSound
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Mark, Marked, RangeOf, Strike, StripArmour, StripShield
  • Marks it puts on dudes: no-armour, no-shield, scrubbed
  • Marks it reads: scrubbed

  • Sight: a straight line from its eye to the dude's chest, clear of scenery and table blockers
  • Self-driven: the engine never aims or fires it; its Update hook runs it.
  • Aims ahead for a shot at 95 cm/s.
  • No coverage goal: it stands on the road or only buffs, so the table check does not ask it to reach the road.
  • Bot placement: reach 9 cm.

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • Self-driven: every 0.75 s (divided by its aura rate) it pecks the nearest living dude within 5.5 cm (flat distance) of where it floats.
  • A peck is one strike for the line's card damage, travelling flat from the duck to the dude.

Targeting

  • Its beat is centred on the road point nearest its tile (all routes, sampled every 2 cm) and runs 9 cm either side, shortened near the ends of the road.
  • With nobody near, it floats the beat back and forth, one round trip every 9 s.
  • When a dude on its route (skipping walkers flagged Driven) is within the beat plus 5.5 cm, it paddles towards the nearest such dude at up to 7 cm/s, staying on its beat.
  • The peck itself takes the nearest dude within reach on any route, with no sight check.

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Damage reads the line's Damage stat; peck reach is the fixed 5.5 cm and the Range stat is ignored.
  • The fire rate stat and fire-rate cards are ignored; only the aura rate (neighbour buffs) speeds it up.
  • Repeats are ignored.
  • Pecks go through Strike, so card on-hit effects and Force apply.

Quirks

  • The mini itself moves onto the road; it only reaches the road it floats over.
  • It keeps pecking while the towers' fire is held: it never asks whether it may shoot.

Hooks, verbs and marks (from the code)

  • Hooks it overrides: OnSlotRemoved, Update
  • Engine verbs its code calls: Strike
  • Named outside its folder in: Cards/Packs/CardReview.cs

What its code does (read from code)

Attack

  • On fire it hits a flat cone of 64 degrees (32 either side of its aim) out to its range from the muzzle (plus half each dude's size).
  • Every dude in the cone the muzzle can see is marked wet for 5 s and struck once for the full card damage.

Targeting

  • Direct: a flock patch hides its target from it.

Effects

  • A wet dude takes x1.5 (+50%) from towers whose base damage type is Cold or Shock (WetRule).
  • Only the first placed soaker (Shower Head or Water Tower) answers, so soakers never stack beyond x1.5.
  • The bonus applies to any wet mark, also ones laid by other towers (Bucket, Watering Can, Water Balloon, Pint Glass Giant, Wet combos).

How it uses its attack line (what cards can change)

  • Damage is f.CardDamage; reach is api.RangeOf.
  • Repeats and Radius are ignored.
  • Hits go through Strike with no direction: card on-hit effects apply, Force does nothing, shields never block.
  • The Wet bonus looks at the attacker's base damage type only: a card that adds a Cold or Shock line to another tower does not get it.

Quirks

  • The summary says Wet dudes are slowed a little and slide further when shoved; nothing in the code does either.
  • The summary says a third more from Cold and Shock; the code gives +50%.

LONGER REACH

Card unitsIn play
